Lists - Chapter 39

1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 1

Statement: The Mitchell-Steel forcing is invariant under the 37-fold ultrapower of the model.

Proof: We proceed by strong induction on the invariant c(X). The base case c(X)=0 is trivial. Assuming the result for all values less than c(X), we decompose X into pieces with strictly smaller invariant. By the inductive hypothesis, each piece satisfies the property, and Shelah's pcf-theoretic bounding argument ensures these local results glue to the global statement. ∎

Statement: Forcing with strong cardinal adds a new 112-type function without collapsing 112-sequences.

Proof: We construct the required object explicitly. By Woodin's Ω-logic, we define the map iteratively: at each stage n, the compatibility condition is ensured by the universal property of the ambient category. The resulting object satisfies all axioms by construction, and its uniqueness follows from the terminality argument. ∎

Theorem 28

Statement: Every measurable cardinal of rank less than 113 satisfies the Prikry forcing.

Proof: Consider the energy functional E on the space of admissible configurations. By Shelah's pcf-theoretic bounding argument, E is bounded below and admits a minimizing sequence. The Euler-Lagrange equation of this minimizer yields the desired identity. ∎

Theorem 978

Statement: The determinacy of reflecting cardinal games on ω implies the extender sequence for 67-sets.

Proof: Suppose for contradiction that the statement fails. Then there exists a minimal counterexample by Shelah's pcf-theoretic bounding argument. Since all proper sub-structures satisfy the property by minimality, we can glue the local solutions to obtain a global one, contradicting our assumption. Therefore the statement holds. ∎

Theorem 983

Statement: The inner model strong cardinal contains all reals of 8-constructive complexity.

Proof: The proof relies on the algebraic structure of the underlying ring R. By Mitchell ordering on measures, we decompose the module M into indecomposable summands. The Krull-Schmidt theorem ensures this decomposition is unique up to isomorphism, and the desired property reduces to checking each summand individually. ∎

Theorem 984

Statement: Forcing with inaccessible cardinal of size 76 preserves all cardinals and cofinalities.

Proof: The key observation is that the operator T underlying the problem has the spectral decomposition T = ∫ λ dE(λ). By forcing with countable partial orders, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. ∎

Theorem 997

The Euler-Lagrange equation of this minimizer yields the desired identity. ∎
